# Complete current repository file index

This index categorizes every tracked project file outside the explanation set itself. Local untracked files, sensitive-file metadata and excluded build/tool directories are captured separately in [the recursive inventory](../onboarding-work/inventory.json) and [exclusions](../onboarding-work/inventory-exclusions.json). The explanation set is listed as a companion artifact, not recursively given its own explanations.

Application inventory: **1118 files**. Each has a mirrored guide. Generated artifacts, lockfiles and media have explicit categories. Empty categories contain zero files; none is silently merged into source.

| Category | Count |

| lockfile | 2 |

| binary | 0 |
| image/font/media | 2 |
| vendored | 0 |
| other | 1 |

| `frontend/playwright.config.ts` | configuration | Source comment (intent): Deviation from the Day 18 plan's literal text, reported per its own "report the difference" rule (Task 18.6): the plan calls for the API's plain-http launch profile. That breaks antiforgery — `AntiforgeryOptions.Cookie.SecurePolicy = Always` makes `GetAndStoreTokens`/`ValidateRequestAsync` hard-check `HttpContext.Request.IsHttps` on the request *the backend itself receives*, independent of "localhost is a trustworthy origin" (that exception covers the browser accepting a Secure cookie from http://localhost:5173, not whether Kestrel saw TLS). This is the same reason Day 16 moved dev to HTTPS rather than weakening that policy. E2E therefore uses the same "https" profile as normal development; the readiness probe below targets that profile's plain-http sibling binding purely to dodge the self-signed certificate during the Node-side health check (the API itself is still reached over HTTPS). | [Guide](frontend/playwright.config.ts.md) |

| `frontend/src/components/shared/PhoneInput.tsx` | source | Source comment (intent): A telephone input that is always left-to-right, even inside an Arabic form: a phone number has its own reading direction regardless of the surrounding page's, and without `dir="ltr"` the bidi algorithm reorders its digits and leading "+" in an RTL document. Carries no formatting or validation of its own beyond what the browser's own `type="tel"` offers — the server owns the real format (Day 41). | [Guide](frontend/src/components/shared/PhoneInput.tsx.md) |
| `frontend/src/components/shared/PhoneText.test.tsx` | test | Test/support source. Read its assertions and fixture setup together; test source is not evidence that the tests were executed. | [Guide](frontend/src/components/shared/PhoneText.test.tsx.md) |
| `frontend/src/components/shared/PhoneText.tsx` | source | Source comment (intent): Renders a phone number isolated as left-to-right text (Day 41): without `dir="ltr"`, a value like "+201012345678" sitting inside Arabic text gets its leading "+" reordered to the wrong end by the bidi algorithm, because the surrounding paragraph's direction, not the number's own, decides how an undirected run of digits and punctuation is laid out. `asLink` renders a `tel:` link for a phone meant to be called directly (a parent's contact number); plain text otherwise (a value shown for reference only). | [Guide](frontend/src/components/shared/PhoneText.tsx.md) |
| `frontend/src/components/shared/SearchInput.test.tsx` | test | Test/support source. Read its assertions and fixture setup together; test source is not evidence that the tests were executed. | [Guide](frontend/src/components/shared/SearchInput.test.tsx.md) |
| `frontend/src/components/shared/SearchInput.tsx` | source | Source comment (intent): A search box controlled by a URL search parameter (Day 41): the caller owns `value` (read from the route's search) and `onChange` (navigates with the new term). Typing updates the visible text immediately but only calls `onChange` after `debounceMs` of no further typing, so the server is never asked once per keystroke. Clearing is immediate — a deliberate action, not something to debounce. | [Guide](frontend/src/components/shared/SearchInput.tsx.md) |
| `frontend/src/components/shared/StatTile.tsx` | source | Source comment (intent): One summary figure above a list ("Active teachers: 4"). The value is a plain number rendered left-to-right with Western digits, the app's convention in both languages, so it never reorders inside Arabic text. | [Guide](frontend/src/components/shared/StatTile.tsx.md) |

| `frontend/src/components/shared/useParentLookup.ts` | source | Source comment (intent): Owns the parent-lookup mutation and its result, independent of any single form: the registration form and the parents card's "Add parent" dialog each drive their own instance. A result is only ever read against the exact phone it was looked up for (`lookedUpPhoneRef`) — changing the field clears it immediately (`onPhoneChange`), and a response that resolves after the field has moved on to a different number (`latestPhoneRef` no longer matches) is discarded rather than applied, so a later, different number never reuses an earlier lookup's result. The lookup itself is a mutation (Day 42 rules), never a cached query, so the phone never sits in a query-cache key. | [Guide](frontend/src/components/shared/useParentLookup.ts.md) |

| `frontend/src/features/schedule/TimetablePage.tsx` | source | Source comment (intent): One week of sessions in the centre's time, the same page for every role: the server returns a teacher only their own sessions, so this page holds no role check. | `frontend/src/features/schedule/centreTime.ts` | source | Source comment (intent): The one place the frontend turns times into text for the schedule (Day 53). Every function takes the centre's IANA zone as an argument and never reads the browser's: a secretary abroad, or a laptop set to another zone, sees the centre's wall-clock time. `Intl` applies the zone's rules for each instant's own date, so 16:00Z in April's winter time and 15:00Z in May's summer time both read 18:00 in Cairo, exactly as the server generated them. No date library. | [Guide](frontend/src/features/schedule/centreTime.ts.md) |
| `frontend/src/features/schedule/scheduleMessages.ts` | source | Source comment (intent): Schedule refusals are ordinary outcomes of a busy office (a teacher already booked, a session someone else cancelled first), so they get short, specific wording in the "schedule" namespace; anything else falls back to the shared translated message. | [Guide](frontend/src/features/schedule/scheduleMessages.ts.md) |
| `frontend/src/features/schedule/scheduleRules.ts` | source | Source comment (intent): Where a rule stands on `today` (the centre's date): ended once its last date has passed, so a rule ended for next week still reads "Ends …". | `frontend/src/features/schedule/scheduleSchemas.ts` | source | Source comment (intent): Client-side convenience only; the server repeats every rule (ScheduleRule.Create, the validators, the handlers' date windows) and the database refuses any overlap. Ranges mirror the server's: a duration of 30–240 minutes in steps of 5, a start on a 5-minute boundary, a room of at most 40 characters. The time is collected the way it is shown (hour 1–12, minute, AM/PM) and sent as a 24-hour "HH:mm" local time: never an offset or an instant. Messages are translation keys in the "schedule" namespace. | [Guide](frontend/src/features/schedule/scheduleSchemas.ts.md) |
| `frontend/src/features/schedule/scheduleTestData.ts` | source | Source comment (intent): Tuesday 20 April 2027, 10:00 in Cairo: the week of Saturday 24 April is "next", 17 April is "this week". | [Guide](frontend/src/features/schedule/scheduleTestData.ts.md) |

| `frontend/src/features/schedule/useScheduleMutations.ts` | source | Source comment (intent): The one definition of the four schedule writes, shared by the group's Schedule tab and the timetable. | `frontend/src/features/schedule/week.ts` | source | Source comment (intent): Pure calendar-date helpers for "YYYY-MM-DD" strings (Day 53). A calendar date has no time zone: these never read the browser's zone and never convert one. Arithmetic runs on `Date.UTC` day numbers, where every day is exactly 24 hours long, so no daylight-saving change can shift a date. Never `new Date("YYYY-MM-DD")`. | [Guide](frontend/src/features/schedule/week.ts.md) |
| `frontend/src/features/session/Can.tsx` | source | Source comment (intent): Renders its children only when the signed-in session holds the given permission. Hides for usability; never the security boundary. | [Guide](frontend/src/features/session/Can.tsx.md) |

| `frontend/src/features/session/meQueryOptions.ts` | source | Source comment (intent): GET /api/me as "the current session, or none" rather than a request that can fail: a 401 means signed out and resolves to null, so a component reading this query never has to treat "no session" as an error state. | `frontend/src/features/session/refetchMeOnPermissionDenied.ts` | source | Source comment (intent): After a 403 auth.permission_denied, refetches /api/me so the UI's permission-based hiding catches up with a role that changed server-side mid-session. | [Guide](frontend/src/features/session/refetchMeOnPermissionDenied.ts.md) |

| `frontend/src/features/students/RegisterStudentForm.tsx` | source | Source comment (intent): The form the lookup prevents from registering a sibling's parent twice: the client never decides that a phone is valid or unique, it asks the server (via `lookup`) and renders whichever branch the answer picks. | `frontend/src/features/students/StudentEditDialog.tsx` | source | Source comment (intent): Prefilled from the student detail query's own data, not a copy kept in this component's state: `values` stays tied to `student` while the dialog is open, so closing and reopening (or a background refetch) always shows the latest loaded row, never a stale snapshot from the moment the dialog first mounted. | [Guide](frontend/src/features/students/StudentEditDialog.tsx.md) |

| `frontend/src/features/students/registerStudentSchema.ts` | source | Source comment (intent): Client-side convenience only; the server repeats every rule (RegisterStudentValidator, Student.Register, Parent.Create, PhoneNumber.Create). Lengths mirror the server's own (Student.NameMaxLength = 120, Student.SchoolYearMaxLength = 40, Parent.NameMaxLength = 120, PhoneMaxRawLength = 30); shape only, never a phone-format rule — the server decides what a valid phone looks like. The parent's full name is intentionally not required here: whether it is needed at all depends on the lookup's result, which this schema has no way to know. The form checks that presence itself, right before submitting, once the lookup has resolved. | [Guide](frontend/src/features/students/registerStudentSchema.ts.md) |

| `frontend/src/lib/bidi.ts` | source | Source comment (intent): Wraps a left-to-right value (a correlation ID, say) in Unicode's left-to-right isolate (U+2066 … U+2069) before it is interpolated into a translated sentence. Without it, an ID that starts with digits is reordered against the Arabic label around it by the bidi algorithm; with it, the ID reads as one left-to-right run and the sentence keeps its own direction. Plain text, so it works inside any `t()` call; the audit page's `<span dir="ltr">` is the same idea for markup. | [Guide](frontend/src/lib/bidi.ts.md) |

| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/AssemblyMarker.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Lets tests locate this assembly via typeof(AssemblyMarker).Assembly. Contains no logic. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/AssemblyMarker.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/ActorMiddleware.cs` | source | This is the HTTP claims-to-Application identity translation point. It builds a staff actor only from a parseable user ID and a valid centre/role pairing, leaves malformed sessions without a trusted actor, and invokes the next middleware. Handlers consume ICurrentActor rather than parsing claims independently. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/ActorMiddleware.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/AntiforgeryEndpointFilter.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Applied once to the whole /api group: every request whose method is not GET, HEAD or OPTIONS must carry a valid antiforgery token, login included — this is what stops login CSRF (an attacker signing a victim into the attacker's own account). Minimal APIs only validate antiforgery automatically for form-bound endpoints; this JSON API needs the explicit check. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/AntiforgeryEndpointFilter.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/AntiforgerySetup.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Double-submit CSRF protection: the browser sends the antiforgery cookie automatically, but the header value must be read by our own script from our own origin (see AntiforgeryEndpointFilter for enforcement). | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/AntiforgerySetup.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/AntiforgeryTokenResponse.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): The request token only — the cookie half of the pair is set directly on the response, never in this body. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/AntiforgeryTokenResponse.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/AuthEndpoints.cs` | source | Transport orchestration handles antiforgery token issue, credentials, ticket creation, logout, profile, centre selection, and password change through application ports. Login suppresses auto-selection when password replacement is pending; password change issues a new centreless ticket. Identity and membership rules live behind ports rather than direct infrastructure dependencies. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/AuthEndpoints.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/AuthenticationSetup.cs` | source | The setup configures the secure HttpOnly host-prefixed session cookie, eight-hour sliding ticket lifetime, per-request validation hook, API-style 401/403 behavior, and authenticated fallback policy. Cache options are bound here; database key persistence is registered in Infrastructure. Cookie setting changes must respect browser prefix rules and existing client behavior. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/AuthenticationSetup.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/ChangePasswordRateLimiting.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Caps password-change attempts per signed-in user (Day 30 contract): unlike `LoginRateLimiting`, which guards against spraying many accounts from one source before any session exists, this request always carries a session — the right key is who is acting, not where from. Forwarded-header-aware per-IP limiting is Month 2 (Day 34); this does not need it. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/ChangePasswordRateLimiting.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/LoginRateLimiting.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Caps login attempts per client IP: a source cannot try passwords against many accounts without limit. Lockout (Infrastructure/Identity) protects one account; this protects against spraying many accounts from one source. Forwarded-header-aware client IPs are Month 2. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/LoginRateLimiting.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/LoginRequest.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Login is not a CQRS command: the request model and its validator live with the endpoint, not Application. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/LoginRequest.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/RateLimitRejection.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): The one 429 response every rate-limited policy produces. RateLimiterOptions.OnRejected is a single delegate, not a per-policy list: two policies calling AddRateLimiter each with their own handler would have the later registration silently overwrite the earlier one, so both share this instead. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/RateLimitRejection.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/RequestValidation.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Turns a FluentValidation result into the same validation.failed shape the dispatcher produces for CQRS requests (Dispatcher.cs), so a non-CQRS request like login fails exactly like every other validation failure. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/RequestValidation.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/SelectCentreRequest.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): The centre ID here is only a request — nothing is written into the session before GetActiveMembershipQuery confirms the current user actually belongs to it. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/SelectCentreRequest.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/SessionClaims.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Claim names for the session cookie, defined once and read nowhere else but here and ActorMiddleware. Minimal by design: no email, no display name (those come from GET /api/me), no permissions (Month 2 resolves them server-side) — fewer claims means less to leak and less to go stale. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/SessionClaims.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/SessionRevalidationHandler.cs` | source | The cookie validation hook reads user, stamp, selected centre, and role before an Application actor exists. It caches positive query results by that tuple, rejects invalid principals, and clears invalid cookies. Role freshness now participates alongside stamp and membership activity. The positive cache creates a bounded delay rather than instant revocation. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/SessionRevalidationHandler.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/SessionValidationOptions.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Bound from configuration key SessionValidation:CacheDuration (e.g. "00:01:00"). Zero disables caching. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Auth/SessionValidationOptions.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/AppDbContextFactory.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Lets `dotnet ef` create `AppDbContext` at design time, from ConnectionStrings:PostgresMigrations (the tutoring_owner login) through the same `AppDbContextOptionsConfigurator` the runtime migration path uses. EF Core tooling finds this automatically and skips building the full host for it. Tooling, like the seed command alongside it: the one other place in Api allowed to reference Infrastructure directly (S7). | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/AppDbContextFactory.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/DemoModeProvider.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Adapts the bound `DemoOptions` to the Application layer's `IDemoModeProvider` port. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/DemoModeProvider.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/DemoOptions.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Whether this deployment is a demo instance (Task 34.7). Defaults to false: a Production container is safe by default, and only an operator who deliberately wants demo data on a throwaway instance opts in. Changes nothing except the seed command's gate — the demo banner and reporting it in system info are Day 35. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/DemoOptions.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/MigrateCommand.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Applies pending migrations using ConnectionStrings:PostgresMigrations (tutoring_owner) and exits. A deploy pipeline step, not something the long-running web process ever does itself (Task 34.7): owner credentials belong to a short-lived job, not a process that serves the internet. Idempotent — a database already up to date is a no-op, since `MigrationRunner.ApplyMigrationsAsync` only ever applies what is pending. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/MigrateCommand.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/ResetPasswordCommand.cs` | source | The operator-only command creates an Identity scope, finds the requested account, removes its old password, then attempts to add the new password and returns an exit code. There is no explicit transaction around removal and addition in the inspected method; a rejected new password can therefore leave the old password already removed. Treat this ordering as an operational risk rather than a generic atomic reset promise. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/ResetPasswordCommand.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/SeedCommand.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Creates the two demo centres and their demo subjects through the dispatcher. A CLI is just another client of the Application layer. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/SeedCommand.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/SeedGate.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Whether the seed command may run here (Task 34.7): Development and Testing always allow it (so local setup and the test suite are unaffected); Production allows it only for a demo instance that opted in explicitly. Checked by Program's CLI dispatch before `SeedCommand.RunAsync`, not inside it — the command itself keeps doing exactly what it always did, unconditionally, for every caller (including this project's own `SeedCommandTests`, which exercise it directly and never want it gated). | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Cli/SeedGate.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/AuditEndpoints.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Audit endpoints (Day 32 contract: docs/architecture/api-conventions.md). Bind → dispatch → map the result. The centre never comes from the route or the query string — only from the actor. Read-only: there is no write endpoint for audit. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/AuditEndpoints.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/CentreSettingsEndpoints.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Centre settings endpoints (Day 32 contract: docs/architecture/api-conventions.md). Bind → dispatch → map the result. Neither route carries an id: the centre is always the actor's own. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/CentreSettingsEndpoints.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/EnrollmentEndpoints.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Enrollment endpoints (Day 49 contract). Each endpoint: bind → dispatch → map the result. No rule, repository, entity, tenant, role or scope logic: the handlers resolve every ID inside the actor's centre and apply the actor's teacher scope to the two reads, so one route serves owners, secretaries and teachers alike. Enrolling is a nested resource of the group: the group comes from the route only, never from the body. Withdraw and transfer are version-guarded actions on the enrollment (Month 2's convention), never an HTTP DELETE: an ended enrollment stays as history. Transfer is one endpoint dispatching one command, so ending the old enrollment and starting the new one commit together in the dispatcher's one transaction. The student's enrollment list sits under /api/students because that is where a client looks, but it is an enrollment read with enrollment's own permission and contract; GET /api/students/{id} never embeds it. Protected by the authorization fallback policy; none is anonymous. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/EnrollmentEndpoints.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/GroupEndpoints.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Group endpoints (Day 45 contract). Each endpoint: bind → dispatch → map the result. No rule, repository, entity, tenant, role or scope logic: one route serves owners, secretaries and teachers alike, and the query handlers apply the actor's teacher scope below this layer, so there is no "my groups" route to bypass. Name, capacity and teacher are separate sub-resources with their own request models; none of them accepts a subject ID, so the subject fixed at creation cannot be changed through any route. Protected by the authorization fallback policy; none is anonymous. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/GroupEndpoints.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/ParentEndpoints.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Parent endpoints (Day 39 contract). Each endpoint: bind → dispatch → map the result. No rule, repository, entity or tenant logic — the tenant comes only from the actor, through the dispatcher's own layer 1 step. Protected by the authorization fallback policy; none is anonymous. No list and no delete: Month 3 reaches a parent through a student or through the phone lookup. The lookup is a POST (phone numbers never belong in a URL, proxy log or browser history) but still dispatches a query on the read-only pipeline; its response gets "Cache-Control: no-store" the same automatic way every /api response does (`TutoringCentre.Api.Http.SecurityHeadersSetup`), not from anything written here. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/ParentEndpoints.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/PlatformEndpoints.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Platform-level endpoints. Each endpoint: bind → dispatch → map. No logic, no database access, no outcome `if`s. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/PlatformEndpoints.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/ScheduleEndpoints.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Schedule endpoints (Day 53 contract). Each endpoint: bind → dispatch → map the result. No rule, repository, entity, tenant, role or scope logic: the handlers resolve every ID inside the actor's centre and apply the actor's teacher scope to the two reads, so one route serves owners, secretaries and teachers alike. Rules travel as local values only — a weekday, an "HH:mm" time, a duration and "YYYY-MM-DD" dates in the centre's own calendar — and sessions come back as UTC instants with the zone to show them in. A rule carries no teacher: its sessions are taught by the group's teacher. Ending a rule and cancelling a session are version-guarded actions, never an HTTP DELETE: both stay as history. Protected by the authorization fallback policy; none is anonymous. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/ScheduleEndpoints.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/StaffEndpoints.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Staff endpoints (Day 30 contract: docs/architecture/api-conventions.md). Each endpoint: bind → dispatch → map the result. The centre never comes from the route or the body — only from the actor, through the dispatcher's tenant step. Protected by the authorization fallback policy; none is anonymous. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/StaffEndpoints.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/StudentEndpoints.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Student endpoints (Day 41 contract: docs/architecture/api-conventions.md). Each endpoint: bind → dispatch → map the result. No rule, repository, entity or tenant logic — the tenant comes only from the actor, through the dispatcher's own layer 1 step. Protected by the authorization fallback policy; none is anonymous. Unlink and make-primary are action sub-resources carrying the student's version in the body, following Month 2's convention for state changes, instead of an HTTP DELETE with a body: the parent id comes from the route and the version from the body, so the two cannot disagree about which student is being changed. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/StudentEndpoints.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/SubjectEndpoints.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Subject endpoints (Day 26 contract: docs/architecture/api-conventions.md). Each endpoint: bind → dispatch → map the result. No rule, repository, entity or tenant logic — the tenant comes only from the actor, through the dispatcher's own layer 1 step. Protected by the authorization fallback policy; none is anonymous. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/SubjectEndpoints.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/TeacherEndpoints.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Teacher endpoints (Day 43 contract). Each endpoint: bind → dispatch → map the result. No rule, repository, entity or tenant logic — the tenant comes only from the actor, through the dispatcher's own layer 1 step. The account link is its own sub-resource (PUT .../account): a different decision from editing a name, with a different audit meaning, so it gets its own request model and `userId` is never accepted anywhere else. Protected by the authorization fallback policy; none is anonymous. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Endpoints/TeacherEndpoints.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/CorrelationIdMiddleware.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Resolves or creates the request's correlation ID, exposes it on the response and in every log line. A client-supplied value is accepted only when it is 1–64 characters of letters, digits, '.', '_' or '-': an unvalidated header written into logs could inject fake log lines or huge values. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/CorrelationIdMiddleware.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/ForwardedHeadersSetup.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Lets the app learn the real client address and scheme when it sits behind a reverse proxy, trusting that information only from configured, known proxy networks (Task 34.3) — a header is a claim, only evidence when it comes from a machine this app is told to trust. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/ForwardedHeadersSetup.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/GlobalExceptionHandler.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): The one place unexpected exceptions are logged and translated. Responses never contain exception text, types or stack traces (information leakage); the full exception goes to the log only. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/GlobalExceptionHandler.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/HourMinuteTimeOnlyJsonConverter.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): A local time of day as exactly "HH:mm" (24-hour), both ways. The framework default writes "HH:mm:ss" and also reads seconds; the API contract has neither. Anything else — seconds, an offset, a UTC instant — fails to read, which the strict-JSON pipeline turns into 400 request.malformed. The OpenAPI schema is unaffected: it is built from the CLR type, so a TimeOnly still documents as a string with the "time" format. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/HourMinuteTimeOnlyJsonConverter.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/HttpContextKeys.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Keys for per-request values stored in `HttpContext.Items`. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/HttpContextKeys.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/ProblemDetailsSetup.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Registers Problem Details for framework-generated errors (empty 404/405, exception handler fallbacks). | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/ProblemDetailsSetup.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/ProblemResult.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Writes a Problem Details body (RFC 9457) with the standard extensions. The single writer for every error response. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/ProblemResult.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/ProxyOptions.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Which reverse-proxy networks' X-Forwarded-For/X-Forwarded-Proto headers are trusted (Task 34.3). A proxy header is a claim; it is only evidence when it comes from a machine this list names. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/ProxyOptions.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/ProxyOptionsValidator.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): A misconfigured container must fail at startup, not at first request (Task 34.7's options-validation goal applied here too). The "empty list" rule is Production-only and a CIDR syntax check applies everywhere — neither is expressible with a data-annotation attribute, since the first needs `IHostEnvironment`. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/ProxyOptionsValidator.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/ResultHttpExtensions.cs` | source | The shared transport mapper converts result kinds to status and Problem Details while preserving field dictionaries for any kind, including conflicts. Creation helpers support both Location-bearing and body-only 201 contracts. Feature endpoints choose the success shape; they do not duplicate failure switches. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/ResultHttpExtensions.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/SecurityHeadersSetup.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Browser-enforced defence in depth: every response (HTML, hashed assets, `/api` responses, and error responses alike) carries the same headers, set as early as possible so nothing downstream can skip them. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/SecurityHeadersSetup.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/SpaHostingSetup.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Serves the frontend's production build from the web root, so the browser sees one origin (no CORS, and `__Host-` cookies that simply work). | `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/UtcInstantJsonConverter.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Every instant leaves the API as ISO 8601 in UTC with "Z" (Day 53 contract), whatever offset the value carries; the framework default writes the value's own offset ("+00:00" for what PostgreSQL returns). Reading is the framework's own ISO 8601 parsing, unchanged. The OpenAPI schema keeps the "date-time" format through the schema transformer in Program.cs, since a custom converter hides the JSON type from the schema exporter. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Http/UtcInstantJsonConverter.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Logging/SensitiveDataDestructuringPolicy.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Safety net: when an object is destructured ({@Request}), properties named like Password, Token, Secret, ConnectionString or Phone* are replaced with "***". | `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Program.cs` | source | The process composition root registers application and infrastructure services, JSON contracts, telemetry, authentication, and request protections. It selects migrate, seed, and reset-password CLI paths before HTTP execution, then installs the ordered middleware chain and maps all feature endpoint groups. Static files precede explicit routing; trusted forwarded headers run before scheme- and client-dependent behavior. Changes here affect every request and the build-time OpenAPI host. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Program.cs.md) |

| `src/TutoringCentre.Api/Telemetry/TelemetrySetup.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Traces, metrics and logs, wired only in this project's composition root (Task 35.6): Domain and Application gain no telemetry dependency. Off entirely unless configuration key Otel:Endpoint is set, so Development and the test suite export nothing and never attempt a network call. The manual's own design targets the Azure Monitor exporter once Azure is provisioned (deferred, no subscription yet); until then this points at a plain OTLP endpoint — a local viewer such as the .NET Aspire Dashboard, or any other OTLP collector — so the same instrumentation is exercised without any cloud resource. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Api/Telemetry/TelemetrySetup.cs.md) |

One timestamp ends the source as Transferred and starts the destination. Only the destination needs a capacity lock, avoiding opposing-transfer lock cycles. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Application/Academics/Enrollments/Commands/TransferEnrollment/TransferEnrollmentHandler.cs.md) |

| `src/TutoringCentre.Domain/Academics/EnrollmentEndReason.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Why an enrollment ended. Rules end once rather than editing recurrence history in place. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Domain/Academics/Scheduling/ScheduleRule.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Domain/Academics/Scheduling/SessionGenerator.cs` | source | The pure generator evaluates each local date separately, applies explicit skipped/repeated DST policy, converts to UTC, and adds duration as elapsed minutes. It accepts zone and range explicitly and reads no clock or repository. Repeated UTC additions would break its wall-clock recurrence contract across offset changes. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Domain/Academics/Scheduling/SessionGenerator.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Domain/Academics/Student.cs` | source | The student aggregate owns its parent links and enforces one-to-four contacts, no duplicates, and exactly one primary. Successful link changes increase LinksRevision; archived students are read-only for ordinary edits. Registration creates a primary link immediately, and primary replacement is a dedicated atomic in-memory transition. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Domain/Academics/Student.cs.md) |

A null diff means no audit row, and JSON primitive representation is centralized here. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Audit/AuditDiffBuilder.cs.md) |

| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Audit/AuditEntry.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): One append-only row in audit.audit_entries: who changed what, when, and the before/after values. | `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Audit/AuditPolicy.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): The explicit allow-list of everything this audit log may ever record (Task 31.4): the audited entity types, their stable audit name, how to find the centre a change to them belongs to, and the only properties that may appear in a changes document. A property not listed here is never recorded, however it changes — a new property added to `Subject`, `Membership` or `Centre` stays unaudited until someone decides it is safe to add here. Audit entries themselves, and Identity's user type, are never audited: neither appears below. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Audit/AuditPolicy.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Audit/AuditPolicyEntry.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): One `AuditPolicy` entry: an audited entity's stable audit name, how to find the centre a change to it belongs to, and the only property names on it that may ever appear in a changes document. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Audit/AuditPolicyEntry.cs.md) |

| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Centres/CentreTimeZoneProvider.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): EF Core implementation of `ICentreTimeZoneProvider`. The centre predicate is written by hand, like `TutoringCentre.Infrastructure.ReadServices.CentreReadService`'s: platform.centres is the tenant table itself and has no EF query filter or row-level security, so the only thing narrowing this query to one centre is the explicit centre.Id == centreId, with the id taken from the current actor. The zone ID was validated by Centre.Create, so an unknown ID here is a bug and is allowed to throw. Registered scoped and cached in a field, so the row is read at most once per request. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Centres/CentreTimeZoneProvider.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/DependencyInjection.cs` | source | The infrastructure composition method binds runtime and migration database options, registers clocks and interceptor lifetimes, creates the runtime AppDbContext, persists Data Protection keys to PostgreSQL, wires repository/read-service ports, and configures Identity. Runtime DI uses the application connection; migration construction is separate. Interceptor lifetimes differ because audit reads scoped identity while timestamp and tenant guard are stateless. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/DependencyInjection.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Identity/ApplicationUser.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): The Identity persistence model for a staff user. | `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Identity/DevelopmentIdentitySeeder.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Development-only bootstrapping, not a use case: creates a realistic two-centre staff set directly against Infrastructure, using the Domain's own factories so seeded data obeys the same rules as data created through the Application layer. Never runs at normal application startup or during build-time OpenAPI generation. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Identity/DevelopmentIdentitySeeder.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Identity/IdentityAuthenticationService.cs` | source | The credential adapter uses UserManager for account lookup, dummy verification for unknown email, lockout, password checking, and failure counters. Password change rejects reuse, maps mismatch and policy errors, clears pending replacement, and updates security stamp. Its existence does not establish measured timing equality or cancellation coverage for UserManager calls. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Identity/IdentityAuthenticationService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Identity/StaffAccountService.cs` | source | The provisioning adapter reuses an existing login unchanged or generates a cryptographic temporary password and creates a pending-change account. UserManager uses the same scoped context, so creation inside dispatch participates in its transaction. Existing accounts return no temporary password; new plaintext credentials are returned for the creation response rather than stored as a user field. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Identity/StaffAccountService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Identity/StaffDirectory.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): EF Core implementation of `IStaffDirectory`. The centre condition is written by hand, like `TutoringCentre.Infrastructure.ReadServices.StaffReadService`'s: identity.memberships has no EF query filter and no row-level security (Day 29's documented exemption — a membership is read at login, before a tenant is selected), so nothing underneath this query narrows it to one centre automatically. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Identity/StaffDirectory.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Identity/TeacherScopeProvider.cs` | source | The scoped provider converts actor identity into All, Own, or None group ownership scope and caches that decision once per request. Teacher roles resolve through an active linked academic teacher; missing linkage fails closed. System and unrestricted staff roles get All while unknown actors never default to unrestricted data. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Identity/TeacherScopeProvider.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/AppDbContext.cs` | source | The single context combines Identity user storage and ordinary domain mappings with the platform Data Protection key context. It reads the actor's centre live, applies assembly configurations, allows a test-model extension, then installs tenant filters last. Only the required DataProtectionKeys DbSet is exposed explicitly; adapters use Set<T> for other types. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/AppDbContext.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/AppDbContextOptionsConfigurator.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): The one place Npgsql, the snake_case naming convention, the migrations history table and the save interceptors are configured, so the runtime registration (tutoring_app, DependencyInjection.cs) and the short-lived migration context (tutoring_owner, `MigrationRunner` and the design-time factory) can never drift apart — including which interceptors run on each. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/AppDbContextOptionsConfigurator.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/ClassSessionConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps ClassSession to academics.class_sessions. The start and end are UTC timestamptz instants; the local date is a date in the centre's zone, kept beside them so the timetable selects by calendar day. The group, teacher and rule references are composite foreign keys through (centre_id, id) with no navigation and no cascade: a session is never deleted, only cancelled. The unique (centre_id, schedule_rule_id, local_date) makes generation repeatable. The two exclusion constraints that forbid double-booking cannot be expressed in EF's model; they are written as SQL in the AddSchedule migration (ADR 0012). | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/ClassSessionConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/EnrollmentConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps Enrollment to academics.enrollments. The student and group references are composite foreign keys through (centre_id, id), so an enrollment can never join a student and a group of different centres, even through privileged SQL; no navigation property to either. The partial unique index is the race-proof form of "not already enrolled": at most one active row per student and group, while ended rows stay as history so a student can re-enroll. The checks make an impossible state (ended without a time or a reason, or ending before it started) unstorable even by a bug. Capacity is deliberately not here — "at most N active rows for this group" is not expressible as a unique or check constraint; it is enforced under a row lock on the group (ADR 0011). | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/EnrollmentConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/GroupConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps Group to academics.groups. Both the subject and teacher references are composite foreign keys through (centre_id, id) — the same pattern as StudentParentLink's link to Parent — so a group can never store a reference into another centre's subject or teacher, even through privileged SQL. No navigation property to either: a handler reaches Subject or Teacher only through their own repositories. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/GroupConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/ParentConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps the Parent entity to academics.parents. Lengths mirror the Domain's limits exactly. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/ParentConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/PhoneFormatCheck.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): The database-level backstop for E.164 shape, shared by academics.parents and academics.students (Day 38 contract): looser than PhoneNumber's own validation (no Egyptian-prefix check), since the Domain already fully validates before save — this check only catches a row that reached the column some other way. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/PhoneFormatCheck.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/PhoneNumberConverters.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): The persistence half of PhoneNumber (Task 38.1): stored as its E.164 string in one column, for both a required and an optional phone. Reading a column value that no longer passes PhoneNumber.Create means the row holds corrupt data; that failure surfaces as an exception here, not a Result, since a value converter has no way to report one through EF's materialisation path. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/PhoneNumberConverters.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/ScheduleRuleConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps ScheduleRule to academics.schedule_rules. Every value is local: the weekday as a smallint (0 = Sunday, matching `DayOfWeek`), the start as a time and the effective range as dates — never a timestamp, because a rule says "18:00 in the centre's zone", not an instant. The group reference is a composite foreign key through (centre_id, id), so a rule can never point at another centre's group; no navigation. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/ScheduleRuleConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/StudentConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps the Student aggregate root to academics.students. The parent links (`StudentParentLink`) are persisted through the private backing field behind `Student.Parents`, read and written by EF directly — the aggregate's public surface stays read-only, exactly as Day 37 left it. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/StudentConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/StudentParentLinkConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps StudentParentLink, the Student aggregate's child entity, to academics.student_parents. The relationship to Student is declared from `StudentConfiguration` (the aggregate root's own backing field); only the relationship to Parent is declared here, with no navigation back — a link knows a ParentId, never a Parent object, so no handler can reach across aggregates through it. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/StudentParentLinkConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/SubjectConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps the Subject entity to academics.subjects. Lengths mirror the Domain's limits exactly. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/SubjectConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/TeacherConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps the Teacher entity to academics.teachers. The optional account link (`Teacher.UserId`) has no EF relationship here: it has no navigation and its target, identity.memberships, has no EF-declared key on (centre_id, user_id) for HasPrincipalKey to reference — only a plain unique index, which Postgres accepts as a foreign key target on its own. The composite foreign key is therefore added as a raw operation directly in the AddTeachers migration (Task 43.2), never through this configuration, so the EF model never describes it and the model snapshot has nothing to drift against identity's own model. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Academics/TeacherConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Audit/AuditEntryConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps `AuditEntry` to audit.audit_entries, per the Day 31 contract. No DDL beyond the column shape lives here: row-level security and the append-only grant are in migration AddAuditLog (Task 31.3). | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Audit/AuditEntryConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Centres/CentreConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps the Centre entity to platform.centres. Lengths mirror the Domain's limits exactly. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Centres/CentreConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Identity/ApplicationUserConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps ApplicationUser to identity.users. Applied after Identity's own base configuration, so it wins. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Identity/ApplicationUserConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Identity/IdentityUserClaimConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Renames Identity's default claims table into identity.user_claims. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Identity/IdentityUserClaimConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Identity/IdentityUserLoginConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Renames Identity's default logins table into identity.user_logins. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Identity/IdentityUserLoginConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Identity/IdentityUserTokenConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Renames Identity's default tokens table into identity.user_tokens. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Identity/IdentityUserTokenConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Identity/MembershipConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps the Membership entity to identity.memberships. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Identity/MembershipConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Platform/DataProtectionKeyConfiguration.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Maps the ASP.NET Core Data Protection key ring (Task 34.6) to platform.data_protection_keys — platform-level, not tenant-owned, so it carries no centre column and no row-level security, and is the one place AppDbContext exposes a DbSet property (`AppDbContext.DataProtectionKeys`) rather than Set&lt;T&gt;(): the IDataProtectionKeyContext interface the Data Protection stack persists through requires exactly that. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/Platform/DataProtectionKeyConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/RowVersionConfiguration.cs` | source | The mapping exposes PostgreSQL xmin through the shadow uint property Version, generated on add or update and used as an EF concurrency token. Domain entities need no PostgreSQL-specific field. Versioned adapters must set the client's value as OriginalValue so EF rejects stale updates at save rather than merely trusting a loaded row. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/RowVersionConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/TenantOwnedConfiguration.cs` | source | The shared mapping establishes required centre ownership, a Restrict reference to platform.centres, and a composite alternate key on centre and entity ID. Child composite foreign keys can then prove both existence and same-centre ownership. A plain unique index is insufficient as the principal key target used by EF relationship mapping. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/TenantOwnedConfiguration.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/ConstraintErrorCatalog.cs` | source | The catalogue binds exact database constraint names to public feature errors and field details. It explicitly excludes one-primary-parent and rule/date duplicate constraints because those indicate application invariant failures. Adding or renaming a migration constraint requires reviewing this contract so race losers and friendly pre-checks retain consistent outcomes. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/ConstraintErrorCatalog.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Conventions/TenantQueryFilterConvention.cs` | source | The final model convention finds every ITenantOwned mapped type and adds an expression comparing its centre to the context's current actor centre. It rejects owned and keyless tenant mappings. The expression references live context state rather than freezing a centre during model construction; new tenant entities therefore participate without handwritten query predicates. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Conventions/TenantQueryFilterConvention.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/DatabaseOptions.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Database settings. Validated at startup: running without a database is a misconfiguration, not a degraded state. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/DatabaseOptions.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/DatabaseRoles.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): The two least-privilege PostgreSQL roles bootstrapped outside migrations (db/bootstrap-roles.sh). Named once so grant migrations and the readiness check can't drift from what was actually created. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/DatabaseRoles.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Interceptors/AuditInterceptor.cs` | source | The scoped interceptor snapshots tracked changes, consults explicit audit policy, verifies centre ownership, builds allowed property diffs, and adds audit rows to the same context. It uses one clock instant plus actor and correlation context. Platform bootstrap without a centre is logged rather than audited. Bulk writes that bypass tracking do not receive this behavior. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Interceptors/AuditInterceptor.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Interceptors/TenantWriteGuardInterceptor.cs` | source | The save guard rejects changes to existing audit rows and checks every Added, Modified, or Deleted tenant entity against the acting centre. Modified centre properties are forbidden independently of ordinary business edits. It executes after audit generation, so inserted audit entries receive the same ownership check as business rows. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Interceptors/TenantWriteGuardInterceptor.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Interceptors/TimestampInterceptor.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Stamps the shadow properties "CreatedAt" (Added entries) and "UpdatedAt" (Modified entries) from the clock. The properties are declared in entity configurations, so Domain entities never contain persistence metadata. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Interceptors/TimestampInterceptor.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/MigrationRunner.cs` | source | Migration application requires a separate owner connection and constructs a short-lived anonymous context outside runtime DI. It applies pending schema changes and disposes that context. Its summary caller list is stale because MigrateCommand now invokes it in addition to development startup and seeding. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/MigrationRunner.cs.md) |

| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Migrations/AppendOnlyAccessGrants.cs` | migration | Provides a narrower schema/default/table grant protocol for append-only data: SELECT and INSERT only. Audit immutability depends on runtime privileges lacking UPDATE and DELETE in addition to application behavior. The reverse operation revokes table grants, future defaults, and schema usage. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Migrations/AppendOnlyAccessGrants.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Migrations/RuntimeAccessGrants.cs` | migration | Emits schema USAGE and owner-specific default privileges for future tables, granting SELECT, INSERT, and UPDATE. Existing tables still need explicit grants and DELETE requires a deliberate exception. Reversal removes both default privileges and schema usage, so schema-sharing migration order matters. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Migrations/RuntimeAccessGrants.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Migrations/TenantRowLevelSecurity.cs` | migration | The migration helper emits enable, force, and tenant-isolation policy SQL using the transaction current-centre setting for both row visibility and write checks. It validates table and schema identifiers before interpolation. Policies provide a database backstop independent of EF filters, so runtime privileges and transaction-local settings remain essential. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Migrations/TenantRowLevelSecurity.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/PersistenceErrorTranslator.cs` | source | The translator distinguishes optimistic concurrency, unique constraint, and exclusion constraint failures using types, SQLSTATE, and constraint names. Known normal conflicts become Results; deliberately excluded invariants and other database faults remain exceptions. Unknown unique names receive a generic conflict plus warning, while unknown exclusion names remain loud. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/PersistenceErrorTranslator.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/RuntimeRolePrivilegeHealthCheck.cs` | source | Checks the actual PostgreSQL current_user for superuser, BYPASSRLS, or schema-owner identity and fails readiness with a generic description if any applies. It detects dangerous connection-string configuration independently of whether ordinary connectivity succeeds. Database exceptions flow to health-check handling. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/RuntimeRolePrivilegeHealthCheck.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Schemas.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): PostgreSQL schema per feature module. Every entity configuration uses these in ToTable(name, schema). | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/Schemas.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/TenantViolationException.cs` | source | Signals a tenant write-guard rejection and identifies the CLR entity type for internal diagnostics. It is intentionally not a business Result: uncaught guard violations reach the generic HTTP exception path, because they indicate a programming or isolation failure. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/TenantViolationException.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/UnitOfWork.cs` | source | The adapter begins explicit transactions, sets read-only mode before other SQL for queries, applies a transaction-local current-centre setting, reconciles removed parent links, saves and translates expected persistence failures, then commits or rolls back. Rollback disposes and clears tracking. Its ownership boundary covers handler reads, locks, tracked writes, and generated audit rows. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Persistence/UnitOfWork.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/AuditReadService.cs` | source | Audit reads apply filters and descending occurrence-plus-ID keyset paging, left join only actor display names, and convert stored JSON changes into DTO values. One extra row determines the next cursor. The adapter preserves audit rows without a current actor-user match and does not return actor email. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/AuditReadService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/CentreReadService.cs` | source | Projects settings and the xmin-backed client version without tracking. Centre rows deliberately have neither tenant query filters nor RLS, so its supplied centre ID is the security boundary; callers must take that ID from the actor rather than untrusted request input. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/CentreReadService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/EnrollmentReadService.cs` | source | Builds active group rosters and a student enrollment history through tenant-filtered joins. TeacherScope filters the group before reading a roster; a nonexistent student returns null, whereas a valid student with no permitted groups yields an empty list. The roster cap equals the domain maximum capacity and assumes the write invariant remains intact. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/EnrollmentReadService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/GroupReadService.cs` | source | Applies archive, subject, teacher, and teacher-ownership predicates before a capped SQL projection. Joins supply subject and teacher names; an active-enrollment correlated count supplies occupancy without a denormalized counter. Unknown or missing Own scope fails closed without querying groups. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/GroupReadService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/MembershipReadService.cs` | source | Reads profile, memberships, active-centre membership, password gate, and cookie session state. These identity rows are globally readable by the runtime role, so each operation explicitly filters the requested user. Session state includes the current role as well as security stamp and membership activity, allowing stale role claims to be rejected. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/MembershipReadService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/ParentReadService.cs` | source | Reads parent details and exact canonical-phone matches without tracking. The children query joins filtered links to filtered students, orders by name, and caps children at fifty. Phone value objects are converted to output strings after materialization to avoid unsupported SQL member translation. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/ParentReadService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/ScheduleReadService.cs` | source | Schedule reads apply tenant isolation plus explicit teacher ownership in SQL. Rule visibility follows a group's current teacher, while timetable visibility follows each session's stored teacher. Week filtering uses stored local dates, preserving early-morning Cairo sessions whose UTC date is the previous day. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/ScheduleReadService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/StaffReadService.cs` | source | Lists centre memberships joined to user display name and email, including role, status, version, and the self marker. Membership has no implicit tenant protection, so the explicit centre predicate is mandatory. The application permission check protects this staff-specific exposure of email. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/StaffReadService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/StudentReadService.cs` | source | The list adapter applies archive/search/cursor criteria, retrieves one extra ordered row, then loads primary-parent data for page IDs. Name matching executes through normalized escaped LIKE; phone-fragment matching intentionally materializes tenant-scoped phone projections because the value conversion prevented the attempted SQL translation. Its memory work has a documented scale limit. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/StudentReadService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/SubjectReadService.cs` | source | Projects subject IDs, names, status, and xmin versions with no tracking. Entity filters run before projection; the list excludes archived subjects by default, sorts names, and caps results at five hundred. The cap is truncation rather than cursor pagination. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/SubjectReadService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/SystemInfoReadService.cs` | source | Translates EF applied and pending migration metadata into the application SchemaStatus record. It returns the last applied migration and pending count, preventing EF migration types from leaking through the application port. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/SystemInfoReadService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/TeacherReadService.cs` | source | Reads teachers with optional linked-account display names through a left join and computes active group counts. It deliberately exposes no linked-account email. List results are capped at five hundred, while user-to-teacher resolution requires an active teacher so archived records cannot obtain Own read scope. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/ReadServices/TeacherReadService.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/CentreRepository.cs` | source | Tracks platform centres, checks slug uniqueness, and sets the caller version as EF OriginalValue for subsequent optimistic UPDATE checks. It contains no save call: dispatcher-owned UnitOfWork controls persistence and audit. Centre scoping remains the handler responsibility because this platform entity has no tenant filter. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/CentreRepository.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/ClassSessionRepository.cs` | source | The adapter tracks versioned sessions, projects existing generation dates, loads future or later scheduled occurrences, and takes sorted transaction-scoped advisory teacher locks. It avoids bulk updates because tracking drives auditing and tenant write guards. Existing rule dates provide generation idempotency independently of cancellation status. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/ClassSessionRepository.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/EnrollmentRepository.cs` | source | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): EF Core implementation of the Enrollment write-side port. It tracks entities; the unit of work saves them. No centre predicate anywhere: the EF query filter and row-level security already scope every query to the acting actor's centre. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/EnrollmentRepository.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/GroupRepository.cs` | source | The write adapter loads versioned groups and provides the lock-and-count enrollment operation. Its parameterized FOR UPDATE query names xmin explicitly, then counts active enrollments only after the row lock is held. This operation is shared by enrollment, capacity shrink, and archive to serialize the relevant decision window. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/GroupRepository.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/MembershipRepository.cs` | source | Tracks explicitly centre-filtered memberships and installs expected xmin versions. Its active-owner locking query uses parameterized ADO.NET, joins the current EF transaction, reads FOR UPDATE rows, and counts them in memory because PostgreSQL cannot combine that locking clause with COUNT. This serialization supports last-owner protections. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/MembershipRepository.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/ParentRepository.cs` | source | Tracks parent roots, sets expected xmin on mutation loads, supports exact PhoneNumber equality lookup, and excludes a chosen root during duplicate-phone checks. Add only enrolls a row in tracking; unique constraints and UnitOfWork determine the final write outcome. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/ParentRepository.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/ScheduleRuleRepository.cs` | source | Tracks rules and expected xmin versions. Active-rule enumeration includes only active groups and rules whose inclusive end has not passed the requested date, ordered by group, weekday, and start time. The group check asks whether any rule has not ended as of today rather than whether it has already begun. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/ScheduleRuleRepository.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/StudentRepository.cs` | source | Always eagerly loads parent links for both mutation and ordinary tracked lookup, preserving aggregate collection invariants during unlink and primary-parent operations. Mutation lookup also sets the client xmin OriginalValue. Omitting Include would make valid links appear absent to the aggregate. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/StudentRepository.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/SubjectRepository.cs` | source | Provides tracked subject lookup, normalized-name duplicate checks excluding a supplied entity, and deferred insertion. Mutation lookup places the client version in EF OriginalValue; a stale write is detected by the eventual database UPDATE rather than an early in-memory comparison. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/SubjectRepository.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/TeacherRepository.cs` | source | Provides tracked teacher loads, expected xmin assignment, account-link uniqueness checks excluding the current teacher, and deferred insertion. Its account check is tenant-filtered through the entity query filter; eligibility of the identity account belongs to the application staff-directory port. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Repositories/TeacherRepository.cs.md) |
| `src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Time/SystemClock.cs` | source | Adapts TimeProvider UTC time and the host TimeZoneInfo database to IClock. General FromLocal obtains the zone offset and normalizes to UTC; recurrence generation has its own explicit ambiguous/invalid-time policy, so this utility should not be mistaken for the scheduling DST algorithm. | [Guide](src/TutoringCentre.Infrastructure/Time/SystemClock.cs.md) |
