| `tests/.semantic.md` | documentation | Documentation covering tests/ — Five Projects, One Per Layer Plus One for Architecture; TutoringCentre.Api.Tests — HTTP behavior; TutoringCentre.Architecture.Tests — fitness functions; DependencyRuleTests.cs (type-level, via NetArchTest.Rules); ProjectReferenceTests.cs + ProjectFiles.cs (declared-reference level). Treat historical claims according to the revision described in the file. | [Guide](tests/.semantic.md.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/EnrollmentCrossTenantTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 49.7: tenant isolation for enrollment at the HTTP boundary, every case in both directions. Enrollment joins three resources (group, student, enrollment), so each of the three is the foreign one in at least one case, and mixed cases put one foreign ID inside an otherwise own request — the "everything foreign" case alone would be stopped by the group lookup and prove nothing about the student or target-group lookups. Each case answers the 404 of the first foreign thing the handler meets (group before student; enrollment before target group), and the rows of both centres are compared before and after through the superuser connection. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/EnrollmentCrossTenantTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/EnrollmentEndpointTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 49.6: the five enrollment endpoints' contract through the full HTTP pipeline — status, code, body shape and headers, never messages. Every test builds its own subjects, teachers, groups, students and enrollments through the earlier endpoints over HTTP, so each one also exercises them, and the seeded demo data never decides a result. Every error row of the Day 49 outcome table has its own case. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/EnrollmentEndpointTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/EnrollmentTeacherScopeTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 49.7: the ownership guard on enrollment, as black-box behaviour through teacher@both.test's real session. The roster inherits its group's rule: 200 for the own group, 404 (never 403) for another teacher's. A student enrolled in both an own and another teacher's group shows the teacher only the own enrollment. Writes are 403 even on the own group, because permission is evaluated before ownership, and they change nothing. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/EnrollmentTeacherScopeTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/GroupCrossTenantTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 45.7: tenant isolation for groups at the HTTP boundary, every case in both directions. Ordinary probes attack the victim's group directly on all six ID routes with its real current version, so a 409 could never mask a missing guard. Reference probes put the victim centre's subject or teacher ID inside the attacker's own request: the handler must resolve it inside the attacker's centre and answer not-found, with no row written on either side. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/GroupCrossTenantTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/GroupEndpointTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 45.5: the eight group endpoints' contract through the full HTTP pipeline — status, code, body shape and headers, never messages — plus the one outcome Day 44 added to Month 2's subject archive endpoint, and the two outcomes scheduling (Day 52) added to reassigning and archiving a group (Task 53.4). Every test creates its own subjects, teachers and groups, so the seeded demo data never decides a result. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/GroupEndpointTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/GroupTeacherScopeTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 45.6: the ownership guard proven as black-box behaviour through real sessions. The owner arranges, over HTTP, two teachers and two groups in Nile and links one teacher record to teacher@both.test; the tests then act as that user. The unlinked teacher is covered too, because that is exactly where a default of "All" instead of "None" would hide. Reads outside the scope are 404, never 403; writes are 403 for a teacher even on their own group, because permission is evaluated before ownership. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/GroupTeacherScopeTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/LastSeatHttpTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 49.8, the schedule's Week 8 demo through the whole HTTP pipeline: one seat left, ten parallel requests, one success. Day 48's LastSeatConcurrencyTests proved the use case; this proves nothing between the client and the handler (a shared scope, a retry, a cached count) breaks the guarantee. Ten clients, each with its own cookies and its own antiforgery token fetched before the race (five signed in as the owner, five as the secretary), send at the same instant behind one gate. Repeated with a fresh group each time, because a race test that runs once can pass by luck. Counts are read through the superuser connection. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/LastSeatHttpTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/ParentCrossTenantProbeTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 39.9: owner A cannot see or touch B's parents, proven in both directions with CrossTenantProbe, using a real victim parent and its real current version so a 409 could never mask a leak. The lookup and create checks confirm phone uniqueness and visibility are per-centre, never global. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/ParentCrossTenantProbeTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/ParentEndpointTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 39.9: proves the four Parent endpoints' behaviour through the full HTTP pipeline, against the Day 39 contract's outcome table. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/ParentEndpointTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/ScheduleCrossTenantTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 53.4: tenant isolation for schedules at the HTTP boundary, every case in both directions. Each of the three schedule resources (group, rule, session) is the foreign one in at least one case, attacked with its real ID and real version, and each answers the 404 of that resource with the victim's schedule rows compared before and after through the superuser connection. The timetable has no ID to probe, so it is checked by content: the other centre's sessions never appear, filtered or not — not even for the one account that is staff in both centres. The clock is fixed so the arranged sessions fall in a known week. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/ScheduleCrossTenantTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/ScheduleDoubleBookingHttpTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 53.4: the double-booking guarantee through the whole HTTP pipeline. Two staff add colliding weekly rules for the same teacher on two of that teacher's groups at the same instant; the exclusion constraint lets exactly one in, and the other's rule and sessions roll back with it. Each request has its own client, cookies and antiforgery token, built before the gate opens. Repeated with fresh groups, because a race test that runs once can pass by luck. Counts are read through the superuser connection. The clock is fixed, so the winner's rule always generates exactly four sessions. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/ScheduleDoubleBookingHttpTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/ScheduleEndpointTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 53.4: the six schedule endpoints' contract through the full HTTP pipeline — status, code, body shape and formats, never messages. The application clock is fixed at `Now`, a Tuesday ten days before Egypt moves its clocks forward (Friday 30 April 2027), so "today", the 28-day horizon, "in the past" and the session instants are the same on every run and every machine. Every test builds its own subject, teacher and groups over HTTP, so seeded data never decides a result. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/ScheduleEndpointTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/ScheduleTeacherScopeTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 53.4: the ownership guard on schedules, as black-box behaviour through teacher@both.test's real session. A group's rules inherit the group's rule: 200 for the own group, 404 (never 403) for another teacher's. The timetable lists only the teacher's own sessions, and its filters only narrow that scope. Writes are 403 even on the own group, because permission is evaluated before ownership, and they change nothing. The clock is fixed so the arranged sessions fall in a known week. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/ScheduleTeacherScopeTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/StudentCrossTenantProbeTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 41.5: owner A cannot see or touch B's students, proven in both directions with CrossTenantProbe, using a real victim student and its real current version so a 409 could never mask a leak. Reference probes cover the second kind of attack: the attacker's own, legitimate URL with the victim's id planted inside it (a body field or a route segment), which an ordinary cross-tenant probe — always aimed at the victim's own URL — cannot exercise. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/StudentCrossTenantProbeTests.cs.md) |

| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Academics/TeacherCrossTenantTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 43.14: owner A cannot see or touch B's teachers, proven in both directions with CrossTenantProbe across all five ID routes, using a real victim teacher and its real current version so a 409 could never mask a leak. | `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Auth/ForwardedRateLimitTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): The login rate limiter already partitions by the connection's remote address (Task 34.4's own requirement: change it only if it reads anything else — it does not), so once Task 34.3's forwarded-header processing is wired up, these prove the limiter is automatically keyed by the real client behind the trusted proxy. | `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Authorization/EndpointInventoryTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 30.8: enumerates the running application's own endpoints and demands that every one of them has been explicitly classified into one of three lists — anonymous, any authenticated user, or the permission matrix (`PermissionMatrixTests`). An endpoint nobody classified fails the build, so it cannot ship unprotected by accident. Expectations are explicit routes, never wildcards or generated from the role map. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Authorization/EndpointInventoryTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Authorization/PermissionMatrixTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 30.8 (extended Task 32.11, Task 43.14, Task 45.9, Task 49.9, Task 53.4): one table of role x endpoint -> expected status, executed over real HTTP against Nile's three roles, now including the seven teacher endpoints (21 more literal cases), the eight group endpoints (24 more), the five enrollment endpoints (15 more) and the six schedule endpoints (18 more). The matrix records the permission decision only: Teacher is allowed on the two group reads, the two enrollment reads and the two schedule reads (and reads a group it owns here), while what a teacher may see is ownership, proven separately by GroupTeacherScopeTests, EnrollmentTeacherScopeTests and ScheduleTeacherScopeTests. The schedule cases run on a fixed application clock, so no case depends on today. Allowed cases assert a non-403 success status; denied cases assert 403 auth.permission_denied and an unchanged database, compared against the exact pre-request state, not a snapshot taken after the request already ran. Values are written literally here, never derived from RolePermissions — that would prove the matrix agrees with itself, not with the contract. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Api.Tests/Authorization/PermissionMatrixTests.cs.md) |

Application and the Api — other than the composition root (Program.cs) and the CLI (which wires up Identity at startup and for the development seeder) — see it only through Application's own ports (IAuthenticationService, IMembershipReadService), never directly. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Architecture.Tests/IdentityRuleTests.cs.md) |
| `tests/TutoringCentre.Architecture.Tests/ModuleBoundaryRuleTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 30.9: keeps the monolith modular without separate assemblies. Three modules exist today, by Application and Domain namespace: Platform (with Centres), Identity (with Staff), Academics. A module reaches another only through its requests (commands, queries, DTOs — explicitly allowed) or an explicit published interface in that module's own Contracts namespace (Task 43.3, e.g. TutoringCentre.Application.Identity.Contracts); it may never reach into another module's repository, read service, handler, validator or Domain entity directly. A Contracts namespace is itself narrow by construction: every type in one must be an interface or a record, and may not depend on its own module's repositories, read services or Domain entities — a contract publishes a question another module may ask, never a door into how it is answered. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Architecture.Tests/ModuleBoundaryRuleTests.cs.md) |

| `tests/TutoringCentre.Domain.Tests/Academics/Scheduling/SessionGeneratorTests.cs` | test | Source summary (intent, not independent execution proof): Task 51.4. Every expected instant is worked out by hand and written as a UTC literal; none is derived with the conversion calls the generator itself makes. Egypt (Africa/Cairo) since 2023: UTC+2 in winter, UTC+3 in summer. Clocks go forward at 00:00 on the last Friday of April and back at 24:00 on the last Thursday of October. For 2027 that is Friday 30 April (00:00 becomes 01:00) and Thursday 28 October (24:00 becomes 23:00, so 23:00-23:59 happens twice). If Egypt's law changes, these dates change and the generator does not. `TimeZoneData_HasEgypts2027Transitions` checks the machine's time-zone data agrees before any failure below is trusted. | [Guide](tests/TutoringCentre.Domain.Tests/Academics/Scheduling/SessionGeneratorTests.cs.md) |

## Companion documentation

The `docs/file-explanations/` tree contains this index, retained area guides and one mirrored guide per application file. The complete recursive snapshot in `docs/onboarding-work/inventory.json` includes the explanation files that existed at audit start. This finite inventory rule prevents creating explanations of explanations indefinitely.
